R5 loaded the v77 environment with Settings readback showing registered Expo/native call push status, a redacted native call fingerprint, `Full-screen call alerts` = `On`, DND/Zen `0`, nonzero ring/notification/media volume, and channel `chilly_chat_calls_fullscreen_v1` present with ringtone audio attributes and vibration. Follow-up proof path `/tmp/chillywood-channel-subscription-proof-v51-followup/` captured creator Money Center Transactions showing `$4.99 channel subscription`, `Paid`, `Sandbox`, and `payout status: not_payable`, plus authenticated non-subscriber route denial with `SUBSCRIBER ACCESS REQUIRED` and no active other-user subscription/grant. RevenueCat dashboard refund for the exact sandbox entitlement failed with `Refunding the transaction was unsuccessful`; Supabase later received `RENEWAL`, `CANCELLATION`, and `EXPIRATION` events for the same app user/product, but the then-current `revenuecat-webhook` stored them as `ignored`. On June 13, 2026, `revenuecat-webhook` lifecycle handling was implemented and deployed with migrations `20260613091417_channel_subscription_lifecycle_handling.sql` and `20260613092100_channel_subscription_cancel_pending_unique.sql`. It now handles `RENEWAL`, `CANCELLATION`, `EXPIRATION`, `BILLING_ISSUE`, `UNCANCELLATION`, `PRODUCT_CHANGE`, `REFUND`, `REVOCATION`, and `SUBSCRIPTION_PAUSED`; `cancel_pending` keeps subscriber access only through an unexpired provider period, and expiration/refund/revocation deactivate the channel-subscription access grant. Old ignored rows were not manually rewritten because they lack raw provider payload. A fresh proof attempt used Google Play Console order `GPA.3353-3923-8017-31040..4`, selected `Remove entitlement`, and Google Play accepted the sandbox refund with `1 order refunded`, but RevenueCat did not emit a fresh signed webhook during the proof window; Supabase still had no post-deploy lifecycle row to process. Active-access readback for the old subscription returned zero active subscription rows and zero active access grants because the provider period is expired. The app and Money Center now rely on effective access/readback safety: stale `status=active` rows alone do not unlock `/channel-subscription/[creatorId]`, and expired provider periods are labeled as expired effective access instead of current active subscriber access. Remaining lifecycle proof is a fresh or safely replayed signed RevenueCat event. Live money remains off and rows are sandbox/not-payable. Channel Subscriptions do not unlock Premium, VIP, Paid Videos, Paid Watch-Party Seat Passes, Paid Events, Tips, LiveKit authority, payouts, cash-out, withdrawal, transfer, platform-wide status, or other creators' channels. Proof doc: `docs/CHANNEL_SUBSCRIPTIONS_V1_END_TO_END_PROOF.md`.

June 3, 2026 runtime update pickup hardening is implemented repo-side and published to EAS Update for Android. Commit `dd0f7f0 Harden runtime update pickup` explicitly sets Expo Updates startup checking to `ON_LOAD` in `app.config.ts` and mounts a root `RuntimeUpdateGate` that checks for compatible EAS updates shortly after launch and again when the app returns to the foreground, with a two-minute resume throttle and a per-update reload fingerprint to avoid reload loops. When a compatible update is found, the app fetches it and reloads into it after current UI interactions settle. Android production update group `02cbd580-7408-453e-ab79-d60b6a9365c1` published for runtime `1.0.0` with Android update id `019e8dcd-c189-720d-a94e-eda03547e3ef`. This improves future tester OTA pickup, but it cannot make an already-installed client run code it has not received yet: testers who remain on stale embedded Play internal build `20` still need either a successfully received OTA containing this gate or, more reliably, a fresh Play internal build with this gate embedded. Validation passed `npm run typecheck`; public Expo config readback shows the production update URL still present and `updates.checkAutomatically` set to `ON_LOAD`.

June 3, 2026 fresh Play internal build for signup/Brand Studio tester pickup is complete as far as EAS and Google Play allowed from CLI. The initial `--auto-submit` flow scheduled Google Play internal testing submission `cf08d9e9-96ac-481d-afbd-349d8389ffd6`; the local wait then lost its Expo GraphQL connection after build completion. A direct retry against the same finished build scheduled submission `51ea9b1d-f00a-4e7b-94f5-f4c665c4f6ae` and Google Play rejected it with `You've already submitted this version of the app`, proving versionCode `21` had already reached Play. Tester availability still depends on Google Play internal-track processing/cache propagation on each tester account/device. Use the same internal tester opt-in/download link; testers should update/install versionCode `21` from Play before retesting signup and Brand Studio.

June 3, 2026 signup failure root-cause/hardening follow-up is remote-applied. The repeated tester screenshot showing `Signup Error / Unable to sign up right now` was not a username availability rejection: live Auth logs around the owner screenshot window showed `/signup` returning HTTP `200` with `user_confirmation_requested`, while direct current-repo Supabase signup payloads with username/display-name metadata also returned `error:null`, `session:false` as expected for email confirmation. The actual issues found were: Google Play internal build `20` is embedded from commit `bd5c69a Harden signup submit error handling`, before later signup fixes `ea4b545 Load random values before Supabase auth` and `d035636 Return to login after signup confirmation`; production OTA groups contain those fixes but Play-installed devices have already shown unreliable newest-OTA pickup; and the backend had no `auth.users` trigger, so email-confirmation signups depended on client-side profile completion after first login and could leave confirmed auth users without `user_profiles` rows if the installed client was stale or sent no metadata. Migration `20260603133500_auth_signup_profile_username_backstop.sql` is applied remotely. It adds `ensure_user_profile_after_auth_signup_trigger` on `auth.users`, creates a public-safe `user_profiles` row immediately from signup metadata, falls back to deterministic safe `user<hash>` handles when metadata is missing/invalid/reserved/taken, audits signup/backfill username creation, and backfilled missing recent auth profiles. Proof after migration: a fresh controlled signup returned Auth `error:null`, no session pending email confirmation, metadata username present, and SQL readback showed the matching `user_profiles.username` and display name immediately; `missing_profiles_last_24h=0`; controlled proof accounts were permanently removed with remaining proof-user count `0`. This backend hardening does not expose emails, does not weaken username uniqueness/reserved-name rules, and does not change LiveKit, Watch-Party, Premium, Money, Player, Spectator, Brand Studio, Clip Studio, or malware scanning. Remaining release truth: testers still need a new Play internal build if their installed Play app is not applying the latest production OTA reliably; do not claim Play-installed signup UI is fixed until build/install proof is captured from the Play-delivered artifact.

June 3, 2026 Brand Studio Review & Publish repair is implemented and remote-applied. The owner screenshots showing `Unable to update that review state` came from a mismatch between creator-owned pending Brand Studio assets and the reviewer-only moderation RPC path. Migration `20260603033000_platform_brand_owner_publish_review_repair.sql` updates `review_platform_brand_asset` so creators can review only their own Brand Studio assets, while Owner/Operator/moderation reviewers retain queue access; wrong-account review still returns `brand_review_forbidden`, and creator self-approval is blocked for malware-detected, scan-failed, or quarantined assets. Client `Publish Changes` now approves owned safe waiting Brand Studio assets before publishing the selected profile assets, then reloads the review queue and shows publish-specific safe copy. Remote data confirmed many owner draft assets were stuck at `pending_review`; rollback proof showed the asset owner can move an owned pending asset to `clean`, and a different authenticated user is still denied with `brand_review_forbidden`. This did not change public Platform gates: public rendering still requires `asset_state='published'`, moderation `clean` or `reported`, and `deleted_at is null`; Profile media, Premium, RevenueCat, LiveKit, Watch-Party, Money, Clip Studio, Player, and malware scanner behavior were not changed.

May 30, 2026 Brand Studio Modern Asset Manager Upload Fix is implemented repo-side on top of `a7e0b56`. Brand Studio remains Platform branding, separate from Profile Appearance/Profile media. Root cause: Brand Studio uploads used the document picker URI directly as an Expo `File` for Supabase Storage and did not verify uploaded bytes, which was brittle on Android `content://`/cache URI flows. Fix: Platform brand uploads now stage Android content URIs to cache, upload through a bearer-auth Supabase Storage REST binary path with SDK fallback, verify read-back bytes through a short signed probe, then create the draft asset row. The Brand tab now opens as a compact Material 3 Expressive-style asset manager overview, with glassy Hero Media, Background, Avatar and Logo, Theme, Scene Presets, and Review & Publish cards. Tapping an asset card opens a modal bottom sheet for Choose Media, Adjust Media, Save Draft, Preview Platform, or Review/Publish status. Hero/background Fit/Fill/Center, overlay, blur, save, and remove controls appear only after media exists; Hero Reel and Brand Mark remain honest unavailable/planned states where public rendering is not backed. Review & Publish now shows public/draft/safety/review status for creators while reviewer queue controls remain role-gated. Draft/pending/unsafe assets remain blocked from public Platform rendering by the existing published + moderation-safe + scan-safe gates. Validation and Android proof are recorded in the lane closeout; `artifacts/` and `supabase/.temp/` remain untouched.

May 30, 2026 LiveKit Simulcast Dynacast Safe Optimization Pass is implemented as a scoped camera-room optimization. `components/watch-party-live/livekit-stage-media-surface.tsx` and `app/watch-party/live-stage/[partyId].tsx` now construct their LiveKit camera rooms with `adaptiveStream: true` and `dynacast: true`; `_lib/performancePolicy.ts` continues to own conservative 720p/30fps/1.7 Mbps camera defaults and explicit `publishDefaults.simulcast = true`. SDK audit found `RoomOptions.adaptiveStream` and `dynacast` are supported, and Audio RED is already an SDK publish default (`red: true`) that is inherited through the SDK merge path; no explicit audio change was made. New guard `npm run guard:livekit-simulcast-dynacast-policy` pins the two intended camera-room call sites, excludes Party Room, standalone Player, Spectator, HLS/VOD, Chi'lly Chat, and token issuer, rejects video-audio publisher markers, and proves `LIVE_WATCH_PARTY_MAX_SPEAKER_SEATS` plus `COMMUNICATION_ROOM_MAX_PARTICIPANTS` remain `4`. Android release proof on `R5CR120QCBF` lives at `/tmp/chillywood-livekit-simulcast-dynacast-proof-20260530/`: the release APK (`205639147` bytes) built, installed, opened Home, opened Watch-Party waiting room, and resolved invalid Live Stage/Party Room deep links to existing safe unavailable states with `0` fatal crash-scan matches. No LiveKit token issuer, publish authority, host/speaker/viewer approval, Watch-Party route ownership, Live Stage ownership, Party Room behavior, Chi'lly Chat permissions/signaling, old-room handling, Premium gate, Spectator token, Player/HLS/VOD playback, codec/E2EE/autoscaling, production capacity, or seat limit changed. May 26, 2026 Spectator to Watch-Party Live Relay Flow is implemented repo-side on top of `4ffd081c6feb4faa276c63338413924d4df0b15c`. Spectator is now a public-safe watch-only launch surface: eligible sources show `Start Watch-Party Live` for content/player sources or `Start Live Watch-Party` plus `Start Reaction Room` for live-stage sources, along with `Watch with your Chi’lly Circle`, Share, View Platform, and Report. Signed-out creation routes through the existing login handoff. Ineligible, private, blocked, ended, or not-ready sources show explicit copy such as `Source live has ended` or `This live can’t be used for a watch party` and cannot create rooms. Migration `202605260003_spectator_child_room_source_links.sql` adds creator/source reuse flags on `discovery_feed_items`, extends `watch_party_rooms.source_type` with `spectator_playback`, strengthens the watch-party room source trigger, and adds `spectator_child_room_sources` plus `spectator_child_room_audit_log` with token/raw-path metadata checks. New Edge Function `spectator-start-room` is the server authority for room creation: it authenticates the viewer, re-checks public-safe discovery state, block/private/Premium/ticket/subscription gates, creator flags, runtime controls, playback record availability, backing broadcast-session public-safe approval, and rate limits before creating a child room. It returns only the child route and safe source ids, with `fullRoomTokenForSpectators:false` and `originalRoomTokenReturned:false`; it never returns the original LiveKit token, publish grant, host controls, speaker credentials, member list, raw HLS URL, or private storage path. Watch-Party Live child rooms link to the source through `source_type='spectator_playback'`, route to `/watch-party/[partyId]`, and the shared Player resolves `source=spectator-playback` through the controlled Spectator playback resolver. Live Watch-Party reaction child rooms route to `/watch-party/live-stage/[partyId]` and show source attribution while preserving the child room's own people/comments/live controls. Existing LiveKit token issuance, old-room handling, Premium gate helpers, Watch-Party Live route ownership, and Live Watch-Party route ownership remain unchanged. Docs are updated in `docs/SPECTATOR_CHILD_ROOM_FLOW.md`, `NEXT_TASK.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, and `ROOM_BLUEPRINT.md`. May 24, 2026 Clip Studio Save Draft Hardening and Proof is implementation-committed at `a716ee1ed5a880922b8d6dc49896655392fc9b25`; final ending HEAD is the docs-closeout commit that records this implementation hash. Starting HEAD was `9dbba53aead86a669dc4f8e890626485ee18e09e`. Changed implementation files: `_lib/clipStudio.ts`, `_lib/creatorVideos.ts`, `_lib/mediaStorage.ts`, `app/channel-settings.tsx`, `scripts/guard-clip-studio-policy.mjs`, and `supabase/migrations/202605240009_creator_clip_studio_owner_cast_fix.sql`; closeout docs are `CURRENT_STATE.md`, `NEXT_TASK.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, and `docs/CLIP_STUDIO.md`. The real save blocker was not UI-only: Android proof created a draft `videos` row, then `creator_clip_edits` failed because the trigger compared legacy `videos.owner_id` UUID to text `owner_user_id` and returned `operator does not exist: uuid = text`. Migration `202605240009` is remote-applied and fixes the trigger with `video."owner_id"::text = new."owner_user_id"`. `supabase/database.types.ts` did not change because this migration replaces a function body only and adds no typed schema surface. Clip Studio now has an explicit save state machine (`idle`, `selecting_video`, `video_selected`, `selecting_cover`, `ready_to_save`, `saving`, `saved`, `save_failed`, `retrying`), blocks duplicate saves with an in-flight ref, keeps selected video/cover state for retry, writes a real video row, writes a real `creator_clip_edits` row, reads both back, refreshes Content Library, and shows `Saved draft` / `View Draft` only after the saved id appears in the owner library. Partial saves retry against the existing draft instead of creating duplicate blank rows. May 23, 2026 Trusted Proxy Network Proof Implementation is implemented, remote-applied, Edge-deployed, runtime-routed, and owner-session proved without enabling fake real-IP trust. Migration `supabase/migrations/202605230005_trusted_network_proof_contract.sql` adds signed-proxy proof fields to `security_request_context`: `network_proof_verified`, `network_proof_source`, `network_proof_version`, `network_proof_error`, `network_proof_timestamp`, and `trusted_header_source`, and updates `get_security_request_context_summary(uuid)` so Owner Security and Audit Explorer can show verified/unverified masked proof state without raw IP. The shared Edge helper now verifies `x-chillywood-network-proof`, `x-chillywood-network-proof-signature`, `x-chillywood-network-proof-timestamp`, and `x-chillywood-network-proof-version` with server-only `CHILLYWOOD_NETWORK_PROOF_SECRET`; direct `x-forwarded-for`, `x-real-ip`, `forwarded`, `x-client-ip`, and `cf-connecting-ip` are treated only as spoofable headers to ignore, never as trusted network proof. Missing, invalid, expired, or malformed proof stores `network_proof_verified=false` with explicit error state. Verified proof can be recorded only as `network_proof_source/trusted_header_source = signed_chillywood_proxy`. The deployed Worker source lives at `ops/trusted-network-proof-proxy/`, is routed at `https://network-proof.chillywoodstream.com`, strips spoofable incoming headers, hashes/masks IP inside Cloudflare, signs the proof, and forwards no raw IP to the app. `CHILLYWOOD_NETWORK_PROOF_SECRET` is configured in both Supabase Edge and the Cloudflare Worker; `CHILLYWOOD_NETWORK_PROOF_HASH_PEPPER` is configured only in the Worker; `CHILLYWOOD_NETWORK_PROOF_MAX_AGE_SECONDS=300` is configured in Supabase; the deprecated Supabase secret `SECURITY_CONTEXT_USE_DEFAULT_TRUSTED_IP_HEADERS` has been unset. Route proof returned `200` from `/__network-proof-health` with `rawIpForwarded=false`, and a spoofed unauthenticated POST through the proxy to `/functions/v1/livekit-token` returned `401 missing_authorization` from Supabase while the Worker stripped fake direct/proof headers and generated signed proof server-side. App runtime config now carries `runtime.supabaseFunctionsUrl`; the default release value is `https://network-proof.chillywoodstream.com`; Supabase `.functions.invoke` traffic is rewritten from the Supabase functions origin to the trusted proxy; the default LiveKit token endpoint is `${supabaseFunctionsUrl}/functions/v1/livekit-token`; and media-storage direct function requests use `SUPABASE_FUNCTIONS_URL`. Auth, REST, and storage remain on the direct Supabase origin. The helper path matcher now accepts the same function path whether Cloudflare signs `/functions/v1/<name>` and Supabase reports the function-local path or full suffix. Remote migration history includes `202605230005`, and redeployed helper-bearing functions are `admin-owner-controls` v30, `livekit-token` v67, `admin-live-ops-fix-center` v13, `admin-live-cost-guard-action` v21, `admin-live-cost-guard-webhook` v23, `media-storage` v55, and payout/provider/sponsor/fraud preflight functions v43. A current release APK was installed on Android device `R5CR120QCBF` over the existing owner session; Owner Security showed Owner Access `Verified`, Current Device `Untrusted`, Emergency Actions `Locked`, and a current-device network row with masked proof only: `73.74.204.0/24`, Mount Prospect / Illinois / US, Comcast IP Services, `Verified network proof`, `Captured`, `signed_chillywood_proxy`, and shortened context `70f61747-ded...df45da`. No raw full IP was displayed, no dangerous Owner Security action ran, and no device was re-trusted. Direct Supabase bypass remains technically reachable until a future gateway/firewall lane blocks it, so proof-required high-risk actions must continue enforcing backend policy. No raw IP columns, public raw-IP UI, client-trusted IP submission, Watch-Party Live behavior, Live Watch-Party behavior, LiveKit token permissions/routing, Premium gates, payout/live-money behavior, room files, or public content/message-row network proof were added.

Migration `supabase/migrations/202605230003_security_context_event_link_expansion.sql` adds nullable `security_context_id` references to restricted event/audit tables for DMCA (`dmca_cases`, `dmca_counter_notices`, `dmca_attachments`, `dmca_audit_log`), Reports (`safety_reports` plus report audit helper plumbing), media/storage (`media_security_audit_events`), Live Ops (`admin_live_ops_action_audit`), Live Cost Guard (`admin_live_cost_guard_events`, `admin_live_cost_guard_actions`), finance/fraud (`creator_payout_audit_log`, `network_billing_audit_logs`, `fraud_audit_logs`), and SQL-only staff role audit (`platform_staff_role_audit`). May 20, 2026 Settings/Profile account polish is repo-scoped. Settings no longer renders the private Recent activity list; notification preferences, push registration, and refresh controls remain there, and the copy now points users to their Profile News Feed. A signed-in user's own Profile News Feed remains the canonical private backed-activity surface using the existing `readNotificationList(currentUserId, 12)` path; public profile visitors still see only public feed entries and not private notification rows. Settings now shows an `Admin Dashboard` entry only when the existing owner/operator/moderator helpers allow Admin Console access, and `/admin` authorization remains server-side/route-side unchanged. Settings also adds an in-app Change Password form through `supabase.auth.updateUser({ password })` with local match/minimum-length validation and no password logging. This pass adds no fake activity, no public-profile privacy broadening, no Premium gate changes, and no Admin role bypass.

May 20, 2026 Login password recovery polish is repo-scoped. The public login card now has a subtle `Forgot password?` action that uses the existing Supabase client path `supabase.auth.resetPasswordForEmail(...)` after requiring an entered email, with redirect target `chillywoodmobile://reset-password`. The mobile Supabase client uses PKCE auth flow so fresh recovery links can return a `code` query param instead of relying on hash-token handoff, and the root layout rescues Supabase's host-style app-scheme recovery link into the in-app `/reset-password` route. The top-level `/reset-password` route consumes Supabase recovery sessions from either route/URL PKCE codes or access/refresh tokens, then lets the user set a new password through `supabase.auth.updateUser({ password })` with local length/match validation. It logs no reset token/link/password and strips sensitive auth params from screen analytics. Local validation passed `npm run typecheck`, `npm run guard:refresh-policy`, `npm run validate:runtime`, and `git diff --check`; a clean local Android release APK built successfully and physical Android device `R5CR120QCBF` proved `chillywoodmobile://reset-password` cold-starts through `com.chillywood.mobile/.MainActivity` rather than Expo Dev Launcher. Supabase Auth hosted settings for project `bmkkhihfbmsnnmcqkoly` were verified with Site URL `https://chillywoodstream.com`, recovery template using Supabase's generated confirmation URL, and recovery redirect allowlisting for `chillywoodmobile://reset-password`; full end-to-end password update still needs a fresh real reset email because old emails keep old links.

May 21, 2026 production legal/policy source is repo-scoped. The generic Settings Legal & Support launcher and short creator policy pages are replaced by a shared 12-policy legal bundle in `legal/policies.mjs`, rendered in-app through `components/legal/legal-policy-viewer.tsx`, generated to the public static legal site by `public-site/legal-site/build.mjs`, and checked by Admin Canary legal readiness tests in `admin-owner-controls`. Each full policy is over 1,500 words: Privacy, Terms, Community Guidelines, Creator Rules, Copyright/DMCA, Support & Account Help, Account Deletion, Premium/Subscription Terms, Live/Watch-Party/Chat Rules, Law Enforcement/Legal Requests, Moderation/Appeals, and Creator Monetization/Revenue Disclaimer. Studio upload/publish now requires the exact creator rights acknowledgement, and live/replay event saves require the live/replay acknowledgement when a live/replay state can preserve room content. The policy text preserves creator ownership while granting Chi'llywood the broad service license needed to host, stream, transcode, display, promote, moderate, distribute, preserve, and monetize service surfaces around content. Attorney review remains required before public launch; this lane does not change Live Stage, Watch-Party Live, Player, Chat/call UI, Premium gates, owner/admin permissions, RLS, LiveKit actions, or payment behavior.

May 20, 2026 Owner invisible superuser and scoped staff permission foundation is repo-scoped. The Owner account is treated as a hidden platform-owner account rather than a public creator profile: non-owner profile/channel/search/discovery/follow/Chi'lly Circle/chat surfaces are guarded by server-side owner-hidden helpers and RLS/RPC updates, while Owner self-access remains available. Owner premium/all-access is represented explicitly as `owner_platform_access` and does not create RevenueCat state or entitlement rows. Existing Staff & Roles moderator management stays in the Admin UI, but server-side role RPCs now require Owner or owner-granted scoped permission (`manage_moderators` / `admin_grants`) instead of broad operator authority; successful and blocked attempts are audited. A backend-only Legal Review/Evidence Export foundation adds reason-required preview/export/legal-hold records and append-only audit rows through a protected Edge Function. This pass does not redesign Live Stage, Watch-Party Live, Player, Chat/call UI, or premium layouts, and does not add fake users, fake stats, fake activity, fake premium, or mobile-exposed service-role secrets.

May 14, 2026 refresh/video/data cost policy is implemented in repo. `_lib/performancePolicy.ts` is the named constant owner for live 720p / 30fps capture, v1 30fps max, VOD free/Premium quality targets, room heartbeat/membership windows, room snapshot/comment fallback refresh, Home/Channel/Platform Platform Studio/analytics refresh floors, and chat typing/read-receipt throttles. Watch-Party Live and Live Stage pass shared LiveKit v1 room/capture options without changing token permissions; Watch-Party Live, Live Stage, and Chi'lly Chat call heartbeats use 15 seconds with a 45-second active membership window; Watch-Party Live snapshot fallback refresh is 30 seconds; Live Stage comment fallback sync is 15 seconds while realtime remains primary; Chat read receipts are throttled. Home/Profile/Public Platform/Platform Studio remain load-on-open/focus/manual surfaces, and VOD quality ladder/transcoding remains pending rather than faked. No Player UI, Watch-Party UI layout, Live Stage UI layout, LiveKit token/registry/drain behavior, D7F spectator HLS path, Supabase remote schema/RLS, Hetzner/HLS storage, billing, payouts, ads, or finance behavior changed.

May 18, 2026 old live room handling is repo-hardened for Live Stage, Live Watch-Party, and Watch-Party Live. Historical `watch_party_rooms` rows may remain in Supabase, but mobile room snapshots/joins now require `is_active=true` plus fresh room activity, and host room heartbeats refresh `last_activity_at` while the active room is legitimately alive. Home/Explore/Title live badges and Admin/creator active-room counts filter on the same active/fresh room rule. The LiveKit token function now refuses inactive/stale watch-party rooms with `room_expired` before minting tokens, and reused LiveKit assignments reject stale server heartbeats instead of silently treating an old assignment as healthy. Existing LiveKit JWT expiry and prepared-join expiry remain in force. Closure proof deployed `livekit-token` ACTIVE version 54, proved fresh temporary rooms `OLRM38` and `OPRM38` minted expected-identity one-hour tokens while active, then returned `410 room_expired` with no token after the same rows were marked inactive/stale and retained for history/debug. Physical `R5CR120QCBF` and `emulator-5554` were aligned on a local current `versionCode=8` proof APK; stale Live Stage and Watch-Party Live links showed unavailable/not-found states, room-code lookup rejected `OPRM38`, and Home showed no live rooms. A follow-up repo pass extends the same liveness doctrine to Chi'lly Chat voice/video calls: `communication_rooms.status='active'` plus fresh room activity is required for call joins, linked-call reuse, thread active-call reuse, room heartbeat refresh, and `chat-call` token source; stale call references are cleared from `chat_threads` when discovered. `livekit-token` was deployed again as ACTIVE version 55 at `2026-05-19 00:24:05 UTC`, and no-auth smoke returned `401 missing_authorization`; authenticated chat-call/live grant proof still needs the local proof account and service-role env vars. EAS Android production OTA group `c7237909-19c3-4b03-801d-344fa7c5d5dc` was published for runtime `1.0.0`, but installed proof builds had `expo-updates` explicitly disabled, so public user distribution of this client hardening still needs a new AAB/Play build. This pass adds `guard:old-room-handling`; it does not delete historical rows, redesign UI, alter Premium gates, change Stripe/RevenueCat/Google Play, touch Hetzner/HLS, add migrations, or change package/signing.

May 18, 2026 Live Stage top chrome cleanup is repo-scoped. The top `Menu` button and its sheet were removed from `app/watch-party/live-stage/[partyId].tsx`; the existing `Live Room` return button now sits next to `Lock controls`, and the bottom `Live First / Live Watch-Party` mode toggle remains unchanged. This did not change room behavior, LiveKit/token logic, Premium gates, route ownership, comments persistence, reactions, Chi'llyfect state, package/signing, Stripe/RevenueCat/Google Play, Hetzner/HLS, migrations, or Supabase remote state.

May 18, 2026 Party Room / Watch-Party Live launch cleanup is repo-scoped. The Party Room no longer renders the Chi'llyfects foundation box for launch users; Chi'llyfects remain post-v1/foundation-only elsewhere unless a later lane wires real processing. Watch-Party Live Player dock now keeps `Party Room`, `Room Comments`, and `Controls` only; people management stays in Party Room. Party Room blocks duplicate rapid Watch-Party Live opens while the shared Player/token boundary is preparing, and Android hardware back from Watch-Party Live returns through the canonical Party Room path. This did not change room behavior, LiveKit/token permissions, Premium gates, comments, reactions, route ownership, package/signing, Stripe/RevenueCat/Google Play, Hetzner/HLS, migrations, or Supabase remote state.

May 18, 2026 Live mic authority and viewer visibility hardening is repo-scoped and the updated token function is remote-deployed. The `livekit-token` function is now the final authority for Live Stage and Watch-Party Live publish grants: client-requested `participantRole` is not trusted by itself, unapproved fresh active members receive viewer/no-publish grants, approved speakers receive publish grants only while eligible, muted approved speakers remain seated but receive no-publish grants, and speaker seats are capped at four total with host first. Fresh membership means active/reconnecting, not hidden/removed, and recent within the accepted room/member freshness windows. Muting, unseating, removing, or losing authority now immediately disables local mic/cam controls and calls the backend participant enforcement path so old publish-capable sessions do not wait for token expiry. Live Watch-Party keeps the existing scrollable 3-column member deck with about two visible rows, while Watch-Party Live Player now uses a 5-column participant grid with up to two visible rows before scrolling. This pass updates guards for token authority, speaker caps, downgrade enforcement, and fallback-not-success semantics, and it does not redesign room UI beyond the requested visibility surface, change Premium gates, touch Stripe/RevenueCat/Google Play, touch Hetzner/HLS, add migrations, or change package/signing. Supabase `livekit-token` ACTIVE version 55 is deployed and no-auth smoke passed; authenticated grant/enforcement proof and real connected-participant disconnect proof remain pending until the required local proof-account/service-role env vars and connected media proof are available.

May 19, 2026 Live Stage / Live Watch-Party / Live-First media-contract hardening is repo-scoped. `app/watch-party/live-stage/[partyId].tsx` remains the canonical people-first live route, and the approved UX/layout, comments, reactions, composer, participant grid, labels, Premium gates, and route ownership are unchanged. `guard:live-stage-contract` locks route registration, layout markers, the Chi'lly Party Members deck marker, composer/reaction test IDs, participant visibility preservation, and no merge into `app/player/[id].tsx`. `guard:live-stage-approved-seats` locks Approved Seats token behavior, unapproved viewer no-publish, approved speaker publish, muted speaker no-publish, stale membership/old-room rejection, fallback-not-success proof, participant enforcement, expired prepared-token rejection, and the local fake-identity Live Stage seat-approval proof. The hybrid LiveKit host wrapper now disables local mic/camera on unmount and authority downgrade, and adds a reconnect grace so transient disconnects are logged as reconnecting before fallback. The backend token function remains unchanged in this lane and was not redeployed; deployed `livekit-token` v55 remains the token authority. No new AAB is needed for this hardening lane because no native package, signing, build profile, schema, migration, or public API changed; release distribution still follows the normal JS/runtime release process.

May 19, 2026 Watch-Party Live Player shared-playback hardening is repo-scoped. `app/player/[id].tsx` now treats the room host as the only authority for shared playback mutations: play/pause/replay, seek/double-tap seek, scrub, speed/rate changes, up-next playback, and room playback sync writes are host-only, while viewers remain synced to the host and keep comments, reactions, Party Room navigation, camera/mic requests, LiveKit publishing, Approved Seats, participant bubbles, and the approved layout unchanged. Player native video load/play promise rejections are caught and logged with safe redacted context so the dev runtime toast `Uncaught (in promise) Event {...}` is not treated as an unhandled promise path. `guard:watch-party-livekit` now covers host-only shared playback controls, non-playback room interactions staying open, and the Player native video promise handling. No Live Stage UI, Watch-Party Live redesign, LiveKit token rule, schema, native package, signing, function deploy, Premium gate, Stripe/payout/live-money, ads, RevenueCat/Google Play, or Hetzner/HLS behavior changed. No new AAB is needed for this JS/runtime hardening lane.

May 18, 2026 manual live proof should use the current local dev-client build/runtime on the two attached Android targets: physical `R5CR120QCBF` and `emulator-5554`, both package `com.chillywood.mobile`, app version/runtime `1.0.0`, and the same Metro bundle from current `main`. The manual proof should look for the deployed `livekit-token` v55 behavior, not stale installed builds: unapproved viewers stay viewer/no-publish, approved speaker seats publish only while unmuted and seated, max four speaker seats including host, mute/unseat/remove disables local mic/cam immediately, Live Watch-Party member deck scrolls beyond the first visible six, Watch-Party Live shows a 5-column/two-visible-row participant grid before scrolling, and fallback/avatar-only UI is not counted as a LiveKit media pass.

May 13, 2026 Chi'lly Chat final behavior proof is closed. Physical Android proof on `R3CXA0DS5JV` and `R5CR120QCBF` passed signed-in inbox/thread persistence, post-migration realtime UI delivery without duplicate rows, signed-out physical-device auth handoff for inbox/thread/call-compatible deep links, non-member RLS denial, attachment recipient read/privacy, regular voice-call connect/audio/hangup, video-call connect/remote media/camera toggle/mic toggle/hangup, and invalid `chat-call` token safe failure. No Chat proof blocker remains; the later RevenueCat Android production public SDK key setup and device purchase proof are recorded below.

Public V1 current-build release proof is complete as of May 7, 2026. No release-proof blockers were found, and no runtime code fixes were needed.
Candidate integration for monetization, Chat tab, and Explore search is pushed at `90ff081ff38b36165d2eb18023c018af821dc747`. Communication compatibility route ownership cleanup is pushed at `fc2894658c25ea744f4be6e579083bc4aed27289`. `/chat` owns the canonical Chi'lly Chat inbox and `/chat/[threadId]` owns canonical direct-thread behavior. `app/communication/index.tsx` remains compatibility-only, and `app/communication/[roomId].tsx` is now compatibility-only instead of a standalone communication room UI. `/communication/[roomId]` resolves active communication room ids to member-visible canonical Chat threads when possible, redirects mapped rooms to `/chat/[threadId]?openCall=1`, and redirects unmapped rooms safely to `/chat`. `openCall=1` may open an existing active in-thread call panel only and must not start a new call. Chat privacy/member checks remain canonical. Bottom-tab Chat remains deferred because `app/(tabs)/chat.tsx` would conflict with canonical `/chat` ownership.
Route-tree utility exposure cleanup is pushed. The exposed and unreferenced `app/data/titles.ts` and `app/lib/_supabase.ts` route-tree utility files were removed. Ops alert automation safety gate is implemented under `ops/alert-automation` as backend/server ops infrastructure only. It receives Alertmanager webhooks, writes persistent JSON jobs plus JSONL audit logs, and stays dry-run by default. Destructive LiveKit admin actions require explicit job approval, `ALLOW_LIVE_ACTIONS=true`, and `DRY_RUN=false`; network shaping requires explicit job approval, `ALLOW_NET_SHAPING=true`, and `DRY_RUN=false`. Unknown alerts no-op safely, webhook HMAC is supported when `OPS_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is configured, approval endpoints require `X-Ops-Approval-Token`, read-only job list/detail endpoints return sanitized data and may require `X-Ops-Admin-Token`, and shell execution is limited to exact scripts under `ops/alert-automation/scripts/`. Optional domain-email notifications are added but disabled by default; they require `OPS_EMAIL_ENABLED=true` plus SMTP env vars, notify only for newly created actionable approval jobs, do not email duplicates/no-ops, and cannot approve, deny, or execute actions. Admin Command Center now has an Ops Alerts tab that documents the safety model and secure-proxy blocker; Approve/Deny controls remain disabled in mobile Admin because `OPS_APPROVAL_TOKEN` must never ship in client code. Local proof passed typecheck, Vitest coverage, build, mock-email runtime webhook/approval proof, and Admin source proof; no real email, destructive LiveKit action, network shaping, Watch-Party Live UI change, Live Stage UI change, Player UI change, Supabase migration/function change, Hetzner HLS change, creator-video flow change, public mobile route change, or production secret commit occurred.

May 20, 2026 Owner/Admin Live Ops Fix Center is implemented repo-side as an additive reliability remediation lane. It adds backed `admin_live_ops_incidents` and append-only `admin_live_ops_action_audit`, a server-side `admin-live-ops-fix-center` proxy that re-checks owner/operator role truth before calling ops, and an Admin Live Ops Fix Center tab showing only mirrored real incident cards. The ops service now classifies Live Stage, Watch-Party Live, and Chi'lly Chat call reliability alerts. Chat calls remain on the canonical `chat-call` LiveKit token surface, with `chat-video-call` / `chat-audio-call` used only as incident display categories derived from call mode. Supported diagnoses include token/signaling failures, join/start failures, caller-joined-callee-missing, no remote media with low or normal relay bytes, audio/camera/microphone publish failures, bad LiveKit nodes, cellular-only failures, Android-only failures, failed GitHub Actions jobs, stale LiveKit assignments, and server-proven stale chat-call cleanup. Remediation remains approval-only and dry-run-first: GitHub issue/PR/job rerun actions require `ALLOW_GITHUB_ACTIONS=true`, registry drain/block/standby/stale-assignment/chat-call-cleanup actions require `ALLOW_LIVE_OPS_REGISTRY_ACTIONS=true`, and restart/rollback hooks require `ALLOW_INFRA_ACTIONS=true`, all with `DRY_RUN=false` for real execution. No production action was run, no code was pushed, no chat/call UI route layout or Premium gate file was changed, no fake participants/calls/stats/health were added, and production Prometheus/Grafana/LiveKit exporter wiring still needs manual proof before live alert rules are enabled.

Admin Live Cost Guard foundation is added for LiveKit/TURN runaway cost protection. It is owner/operator-only, backed by `admin_live_cost_guard_settings`, `admin_live_cost_guard_events`, and `admin_live_cost_guard_actions`, and defaults to `enabled=false` plus `mode=observe_only`. Admin now has a Live Cost Guard tab for status, settings, manual event/action logging, event history, and action audit history. `LIVE_COST_GUARD_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is configured in Supabase Edge Function secrets, and direct observe-only webhook delivery proof passed on May 14, 2026: invalid secret returned `401 invalid_webhook_secret`; a valid safe fake warning payload for `TEST_LIVE_COST_GUARD_OBSERVE_ONLY` returned `200`, inserted one `alertmanager` event with `action_status=logged_disabled`, inserted zero action rows, and left settings at `enabled=false` / `mode=observe_only`. Normal users cannot see or mutate guard data. The only LiveKit runtime hook is a narrow server-side token-issuance guard for Live Watch-Party and Watch-Party Live that is inert unless the guard is explicitly enabled outside observe-only. TURN Spike Protection is documented as non-user-facing infra/admin hardening in `docs/TURN_SPIKE_PROTECTION_RUNBOOK.md`, with read-only/operator helper scripts under `scripts/infra/` for snapshots, burn-rate estimation, and emergency cap guidance. No Watch-Party Live UI, Live Stage UI, Player UI, spectator HLS, Supabase content, creator media, Hetzner Object Storage, billing, payouts, Supabase RLS weakening, or normal-user live behavior changed by default.

LiveKit Egress repo-side software scaffold is pushed for backend/media infrastructure only. `infra/hetzner/docker-compose.livekit.yml` now includes optional `livekit-egress` and localhost-only `livekit-redis` services behind the explicit Docker Compose `egress` profile, `infra/hetzner/livekit.env.example` includes non-secret Egress/Redis placeholders, and `infra/hetzner/livekit-egress.yaml.example` records the server-side Egress config template. External LiveKit Egress D7D proof completed on May 11, 2026. SSH to `chillywood-prod-01` worked, Docker/Compose were present, LiveKit/Egress/Redis were running on-host with host networking, LiveKit and Egress shared Redis at `127.0.0.1:6379`, and the Supabase Edge Function LiveKit URL/key/secret were synced to the host secret store without printing values. The proof account first correctly received `403 operator_required`; it was then given a temporary `operator` platform role for this proof only, and that temporary grant was revoked after proof with a post-revoke `403 operator_required` boundary check. The 2-vCPU host required a proof-scoped Egress admission adjustment in the host-only Egress YAML (`room_composite_cpu_cost: 2.0`) and an Egress-only restart; the config is readable by the non-root Egress container and contains no API key/secret values. A private `D7D_TEST_` room with one CLI proof participant and demo publisher started successfully, `spectator-broadcast-start` returned `test_started` with `livekitApiCalled: true` and a real private Egress id, `spectator-broadcast-stop` returned `test_stopped` with `livekitApiCalled: true`, and LiveKit listed the Egress as `EGRESS_COMPLETE`. No HLS URL was returned, no public playback was enabled, no spectator playback was enabled, no full spectator LiveKit token was created, no mobile/app runtime file changed, and `/spectate/[itemId]` remains metadata-only. The bounded proof artifact is under `/tmp/chillywood-proof-2026-05-11T20-59-14-038Z-livekit-egress-d7d-proof-account`.

D7E/D7F spectator playback follow-up is closed: D7E is proved and D7F is runtime-proved as a public-safe gate. Cloudflare R2 is not the current D7E target. Hetzner Object Storage is the current target, using the existing server-side S3-compatible output aliases. Current proof found `S3_*` / Hetzner output secret names present in Supabase by digest/name only. Bounded Hetzner probes confirmed private default objects stay `403`, `x-amz-acl: public-read` objects fetch as `200`, and exact-prefix public-read bucket-policy objects fetch as `200`. The completed D7E device proof used a temporary nonce-protected Supabase function to ask Supabase to read S3/LiveKit secrets server-side without exposing values, physical Android device `R5CR120QCBF` published two real tracks, Egress returned a real Egress id, the public playlist fetch reached `200` with a real `.m3u8` marker, and a segment fetched outside LiveKit with `200`. The D7F runtime proof used a fresh Android-published HLS object and proved only controlled resolver/proxy playback for a temporary eligible public-safe row. Temporary rows/access were cleaned up, and `PUBLIC_HLS_BASE_URL` remains unset.

D7F adds a narrow public-safe HLS spectator gate without changing Watch-Party, Live Stage, Player, Chat, Profile, or Premium ownership. The repo-side model is `spectator_hls_playback_records`, written by server/admin paths only and public-readable only for eligible live public-free, rights-safe, non-paid, non-Premium-full-room records. `room_broadcast_sessions` now allows D7F-approved public-safe HLS rows while preserving the private D7D proof guard. The `spectator-playback` Edge Function owns public state reads, admin publish/sync/disable, and controlled playlist/segment proxying so normal app users receive a controlled playback endpoint rather than the raw Hetzner HLS URL. `_lib/spectatorPlayback.ts` centralizes explicit spectator playback states, and `app/spectate/[itemId].tsx` renders watch-only playback only when the resolver returns `available`; otherwise it shows honest blocked/waiting/unavailable states. D7F mints no spectator LiveKit token, exposes no raw HLS URL in UI, leaves `PUBLIC_HLS_BASE_URL` unset, blocks private/proof/non-public/protected/title-rights-blocked/paid content, and preserves Premium full-room gates.

V1D2 does not use Admin `runtimeControls.ads_enabled`, does not add working Admin toggles, and does not add SDKs, real ad IDs, provider initialization, real rendering, CTV inventory, fake revenue, migrations, generated database type edits, RLS changes, package/native config changes, or Supabase remote changes.
- Public V1 Hardening H1A 18+ Signup Confirmation is pushed as a no-migration signup-only gate in `app/(auth)/signup.tsx`. New account creation now shows `Chi'llywood is for users 18 and older.` and requires the user to actively check `I confirm I am 18 or older.` before `supabase.auth.signUp` is called.
- H1A preserves existing email/password validation order, loading state, closed-beta copy, Terms of Service link, Privacy Policy link, Community Guidelines link, and existing Sign In handoff. If the checkbox is not confirmed, signup shows `18+ confirmation required` with `Confirm you are 18 or older before creating a Chi'llywood account.` and does not call account creation.
- H1A blocks unchecked signup before account creation. H1B2 now provides backed legal acceptance storage for new signups when Supabase returns an authenticated signup session.
- Public V1 Hardening H1B1 private legal acceptance schema foundation is pushed. It adds local migration `supabase/migrations/202605070001_user_account_legal_acceptances.sql` and pure helper `_lib/accountLegalAcceptance.ts`.
- H1B1 creates the intended private table `public.user_account_legal_acceptances` with `user_id`, nullable age/terms/privacy acceptance timestamps and versions, and created/updated timestamps. RLS is enabled with authenticated users allowed to select, insert, and update only their own row. There is no public read, and the table is intentionally separate from `user_profiles` because profile rows are broader social/profile data and are not the right owner for private legal acceptance timestamps.
- Public V1 Hardening H1B2 legal acceptance storage is pushed. Remote Supabase migrations `202605070001_user_account_legal_acceptances.sql` and `202605070002_harden_user_account_legal_acceptance_grants.sql` are applied, `supabase/database.types.ts` was regenerated from the linked remote schema, and signup now writes the accepted age/terms/privacy timestamp plus version to `user_account_legal_acceptances` after account creation succeeds with an authenticated session.
- H1B2 keeps legal acceptance storage out of `user_profiles`, AsyncStorage, and auth metadata. It does not collect full birthdate or ID verification, does not add first-use enforcement, does not block existing signed-in/admin/test users, and does not change login behavior. If Supabase signup returns no authenticated session because email confirmation is required, the app does not fake the write and records no backend acceptance until a later first-use/sign-in persistence lane is explicitly designed.
- H1B2 hardens public access: anon REST reads to `user_account_legal_acceptances` return permission denied; authenticated users keep owner-only select/insert/update through RLS. May 17, 2026 disposable signup proof wrote a `public_v1_h1b` acceptance row for `cw-v1-h1b2-202605180147@example.com`, confirmed owner read, confirmed anon denial, and confirmed another authenticated user saw zero rows.
- Public V1 Hardening H2 upload/content lifecycle polish is pushed. It touches `app/channel-settings.tsx` and `components/creator-media/creator-video-card.tsx` only.
- H2 keeps the existing creator-video picker, upload, storage, metadata save, Open Player, Edit, Publish/Unpublish, and Delete paths. It adds local-only Platform Studio Content lifecycle status for `No File Selected`, `File Selected`, `Ready To Upload`, `Uploading...`, `Upload Succeeded`, `Upload Failed`, `Saving Metadata`, and `Editing Metadata`, with honest non-percent upload copy because backed upload progress is not available.
- H2 creator video cards now show owner-facing `Published`/`Draft` status from existing visibility truth and `Media Ready`/`Media Unavailable` from existing source availability. It does not add fake processing, transcoding, archive, retry, storage-billing, payout, revenue, or moderation states; it does not move storage, change RLS/storage policies, change migrations/generated types, or alter Player/Public Platform/Profile/Watch-Party/Live Stage/ads/RevenueCat behavior.
- H2 follow-up duplicate-upload UI correction is pushed. Platform Studio Content now has one clear `Video Upload` form, no extra header/empty-state upload buttons, and `Upload Status` is inline inside that single form instead of reading as a second upload box. The correction touched `app/channel-settings.tsx` only.
- Public V1 Hardening H3 security/compliance/moderation hardening is pushed. It touches `_lib/moderation.ts`, `components/safety/report-sheet.tsx`, `app/settings.tsx`, and `components/system/support-screen.tsx` only.
- H3 keeps the existing backed `safety_reports` categories (`abuse`, `harassment`, `impersonation`, `copyright`, `safety`, `other`) and adds clearer user-facing category descriptions. Scam/fraud/unsafe product/ad concerns are routed honestly through `Safety`; undisclosed sponsorship concerns route through `Other` until a later schema pass. It does not add new report categories, fake moderation actions, fake resolve/dismiss/ban/delete buttons, RLS changes, migrations, generated-type edits, admin bypasses, or unsupported legal-compliance claims.

Settings still links Privacy Policy, Terms of Use, Community Guidelines, Copyright/DMCA, Account Deletion, and Support; account deletion remains request/help only, not fake destructive deletion.

## Product Truth
- Chi'llywood is production-grade now; future Codex prompts must be exact and scoped, not vague.
- Profile = person/social identity.
- Public Platform = viewer-facing creator surface at `/channel/[userId]`.
- Platform Studio = owner-only creator command center.
- Follow = channel audience.
- Chi'lly Circle = personal mutual friendship/connection.
- Subscribers = monetized channel supporters later.
- Keep Profile, Channel, Platform Studio, Chi'lly Circle, Follow, Subscribers, and Admin concepts separate.
- Public Platform must never show owner-only controls to non-owners.
- Public Platform must never show drafts, private videos, or unpublished content.
- Profile route display params are fallback presentation only; backed profile identity must win once loaded. Direct creator-video Player access must stay public-or-owner and must not expose drafts/private/unpublished videos through deep links.
- Platform Studio is owner-only.
- `/admin` is the canonical platform owner/operator Admin Command Center route.
- `/channel/[userId]` is the public Platform route.
- `/channel-studio` is the preferred owner Platform Studio route.
- `/channel-settings` remains compatibility and must continue to resolve.

## Locked Business Decisions
- Launch is planned as 18+.
- H1A no-migration signup confirmation is pushed: new signup requires an explicit 18+ checkbox before account creation is attempted.
- H1B2 legal acceptance storage is pushed for new signups with authenticated sessions. The private table is applied remotely, generated database types are regenerated, signup writes age/terms/privacy acceptance timestamps plus versions, and anon table access is denied.
- Free users see ads at launch.
- Premium users see no ads.
- Planned Premium price is `$9.99/month` and `$99/year`.
- Premium uses Google Play plus RevenueCat entitlement `premium`; Premium subscription revenue belongs to Chi'llywood/platform, is not split with creators, and must not create a creator subscription revenue pool.
- Full live/watch-party access is Premium and must not be made free again without an explicit product decision.
- Free users do not get full Live First, Live Watch-Party, Watch-Party Live, or full LiveKit room/token/connect access.
- Free preview was not added. Any future live/watch-party preview must be a separate, explicitly scoped, limited, low-cost, separately gated pass.
- Ads support free browsing.
- Premium supports expensive live usage.
- RevenueCat remains the Premium subscription truth owner. The Android production public SDK key must come from `EXPO_PUBLIC_REVENUECAT_ANDROID_PUBLIC_SDK_KEY`; debug/dev keys do not satisfy production purchase proof.
- AppLovin MAX is the primary ad mediation direction.
- Unity LevelPlay / Unity Ads may be added through AppLovin MAX later.
- Do not build an AdMob-only ad system.
- All future real ad providers must go through the provider-neutral Chi'llywood ad wrapper. Do not make direct screen-level SDK calls.
- Ads launch cap: base active session allows 3 interstitial plus 1 native/feed; after 2 active browsing hours allow +2 interstitial plus +1 native/feed; daily cap is 6 interstitial plus 3 native/feed; Premium sees zero ads.
- Ads must not appear inside active LiveKit rooms, during active video playback, while typing/commenting, during upload, on subscribe/payment screens, immediately at app launch, in Admin, in Platform Studio, in Chat, or in Profile/composer contexts unless explicitly redesigned later.
- Launch app ads are platform money at first. RevenueCat remains Premium subscription truth only and does not take ad revenue. Google Play does not take a subscription fee from AppLovin ad payouts.
- Creator-page ad revenue share is later: creator 70% net and Chi'llywood 30% net. Creator-sold sponsor slots are later: brand pays Chi'llywood first, creator 80% net, and Chi'llywood 20% net. No live creator ad revenue sharing, payout execution, sponsor deal execution, or CTV revenue system exists yet; finance and payout-provider tables are foundation-only.
- Creator Platform monetization is later/foundation-only: Premium creators may eventually sell paid videos, rooms, posts/content, collections, events, VIP access, Platform subscriptions/fan memberships, and merch/products/clothing through their public Platform and Platform Studio Money Center once backed, while free viewers may buy paid creator content/products without Premium where policy allows. Paid creator content and creator merch/product sales are separate from Premium subscription revenue. Creator paid content split is creator 80% net / Chi'llywood 20% net. Tips remain creator 100% of the tip amount/net tip with any service/platform/provider/cash-out fee separate and disclosed where allowed. Scheduled payouts are free; optional instant cash-out preferred direction is 1.5% with no default cap. No live tips, paid creator purchases, merch orders, creator earnings, payable balances, cash-out, payout release, or live money movement exists.
- CTV ads are future-only for Chi'llywood Originals and network-style content; no CTV inventory is active now.
- Storage doctrine: Hetzner Object Storage is the current object-storage target, including the D7E public-safe HLS delivery proof lane; Cloudflare R2 is a possible later public/high-download media direction only after an explicit provider activation lane; Hetzner/OVH boxes remain the direction for LiveKit and real-time live/watch-party traffic.

## Prompt Standard
All future Codex prompts for Chi'llywood must be production-grade:

- exact product truth
- exact scope
- exact route/screen purpose
- exact UI layout
- exact buttons/actions
- exact data sources
- exact empty/loading/error states
- exact permissions/gates
- exact backend/RLS/storage limits
- exact forbidden areas
- exact validation/manual proof
- exact report format

Do not accept vague prompts such as "modernize", "polish", "add filters", "add route", or "improve dashboard" unless every behavior is spelled out.

The repo now has `video_renditions` metadata, `record_video_original_rendition`, `resolve_video_playback`, generated TypeScript DB types, `_lib/vodQuality.ts`, Player-side creator-video resolver integration, creator-facing Platform Studio rendition status copy, and a media-storage authorization path for rendition signing. Creator monetization systems foundation is repo-implemented and remote-applied as a default-off control plane, not live money. Migration `202605140011_creator_monetization_systems_foundation.sql` adds server-backed monetization settings, creator monetization profiles, creator content prices, paid-content purchases, content access grants, creator products/orders, tip transactions, append-only creator earnings ledger, payout requests, webhook idempotency rows, and monetization audit logs. Live proof on the linked Chi'llywood project `bmkkhihfbmsnnmcqkoly` shows `202605140011` local/remote aligned, post-apply dry-run up to date, linked lint clean, live-money flags false, and anon direct money-table writes denied. `_lib/creatorMonetization.ts`, `_lib/featureFlags.ts`, `_lib/platformFinance.ts`, `/admin`, Platform Studio, Public Platform, Money Center, and Player carry the doctrine: Premium is `$9.99/month` through Google Play plus RevenueCat entitlement `premium`; Premium revenue belongs to Chi'llywood and is not split with creators; Public Platform is the viewer-facing creator surface and Platform Studio Money Center is the creator-facing monetization source of truth; paid creator content is creator 80% net / Chi'llywood 20% net; tips remain 100% of the tip amount/net tip to creators with separate disclosed fees; scheduled payouts are free; optional instant cash-out is 1.5% with no default cap. `_lib/paymentRailPolicy.ts` and the matching Supabase Edge shared helper now make the payment rails explicit: Premium uses Google Play/RevenueCat, Android in-app digital creator paid content cannot silently use Stripe, creator-support tips cannot unlock digital perks/access, physical merch can use Stripe/commerce only after provider proof, and payout/cash-out stays on Stripe Connect when later enabled. All live-money flags default false, checkout preflight returns `preconditions_required`, creator pricing fails closed until server flag plus backed Premium creator entitlement, payout requests fail closed while payouts/live money are disabled, ledgers are append-only, Platform Studio Money Center is readiness-only, Public Platform has no-checkout commerce readiness state, Player checks the paid-content resolver before signing creator-video playback, and Admin sees counts/readiness only. No checkout session, payment intent, provider charge, paid status, fake purchase, fake tip, fake order, fake earnings, payout release, Stripe production transfer, RevenueCat purchase behavior, Google Play behavior, Player/Watch-Party/Live Stage redesign, or live-money activation is claimed.

## Staging Discipline
- Work on current `main` only.
- Keep unrelated local dirt out of the checkpoint.
- Never stage `artifacts/`.
- Never stage `supabase/.temp/`.
- Stage only task-pure files for the active lane.

## Creator Monetization Sandbox Closeout Audit

June 13, 2026 closeout audit confirms all six Money Center creator monetization flows now have accurate sandbox status and local/manual proof for their core contribution, purchase, access, and readback paths: Tips V1, Paid Videos V1, Paid Watch-Party Seats V1, Paid Events V1, Channel Subscriptions V1, and VIP Passes V1. `docs/CREATOR_MONETIZATION_SANDBOX_CLOSEOUT_AUDIT.md` is the consolidated current truth for flow status, deferred provider-tooling gaps, RLS posture, route/deep-link gates, Premium separation, live-money state, payout state, and the final BrowserStack regression plan.

Global truth remains unchanged: `live_money_enabled=off`, `payouts_enabled=off`, sandbox rows are not payable, no payout/cash-out/withdrawal/transfer is available, and no creator monetization flow should be described as launch-live earnings. Premium remains separate from creator purchases. Tips remain pure contributions with no digital unlock. Digital creator purchases use RevenueCat / Google Play sandbox paths; Stripe Tips is not used for paid digital access. Paid Watch-Party routing remains Party Waiting Room -> Party Room and does not route to Live Stage. LiveKit token/authority behavior was not changed.

Safe deferred provider-tooling gaps are Paid Videos refund/revoke, Paid Watch-Party refund/revoke tooling, Paid Events refund/revoke plus capacity UI proof, Channel Subscription fresh lifecycle webhook delivery, and VIP refund/revoke plus optional direct client active-VIP write-denial hardening proof. BrowserStack is final regression only and remains deferred until the Play/internal launch-candidate runtime is ready.

June 13, 2026 installed-app auth proof on Play/internal v53 is closed for signup verification and forgot-password reset completion. A disposable `mail.tm` inbox was used only on the phone/local proof harness, and no token-bearing URLs were printed, documented, or committed. Signup was requested from the installed app, the confirmation email arrived, the phone-opened verification link launched `com.chillywood.mobile`, and the app returned to login after verification. Forgot-password was requested from the installed app; the reset email arrived, the phone-opened reset link launched `com.chillywood.mobile` on the reset-password route, the recovery session opened, password update succeeded, the app returned to login, and sign-in with the new password reached Home. BrowserStack remains deferred, and Chi'lly Chat / Watch-Party two-user proofs remain deferred until a second phone/session or explicit BrowserStack approval.

- RevenueCat entitlement id: `creator_channel_subscription`.

- Authenticated non-subscriber UI denial passed after the successful purchase.
- Effective-access fallback passed: stale `status=active` subscription rows do not unlock when the provider period/access grant is expired.

Channel Subscriptions do not unlock Chi'llywood Premium, VIP, Paid Videos, Paid Watch-Party Seat Passes, Paid Events, Tips, Live Stage, LiveKit authority, payouts, cash-out, withdrawal, transfer, platform-wide badge/status, or other creators' channels.

## Signup Play-Build Repair

June 3, 2026 signup was repaired on the Google Play-installed build. The app already had `react-native-get-random-values` installed, but `_lib/supabase.ts` did not import the polyfill before Supabase auth initialized. On Android release/Play runtime, Supabase PKCE signup could fail in-app even though the same email/username succeeded through direct Supabase proof. Commit `ea4b545` imports the random-values polyfill before creating the Supabase client. EAS production update group `4679bd00-d966-4950-b7eb-570e120b3e4d` published Android update `019e8b18-10df-7059-898c-376cad4c6ac9` for runtime `1.0.0`.

Device proof on `R5CR120QCBF` used the Play-installed app (`installerPackageName=com.android.vending`, versionCode `20`). After forcing two relaunches to fetch the OTA, signup with fresh proof account `signup.v202850@example.com`, display name `Proofv202850`, username `@proofv202850`, and password length 6 showed `Success` with `Check your email to confirm signup before signing in.` Screenshot proof is outside the repo at `/tmp/chillywood-signup-submit-proof-20260603/random-values-fix/`. Validation for the fix passed `npm run typecheck`, `npm run guard:android-launcher-icon-policy`, `npm run guard:watch-party-livekit`, `npm run guard:old-room-handling`, and `git diff --check`.
